<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct nonzero locally constructible perverse Hecke eigensheaves with Borel level at one marked point on a smooth projective curve of genus at least two over an algebraic closure of a finite field. The parameter is a Zariski-dense geometric <i>ℓ</i>-adic local system with tame regular-unipotent monodromy. The group is simple and simply connected and satisfies four explicit Lie-theoretic characteristic hypotheses. The eigensheaves have parabolic nilpotent singular support, and their eigenisomorphisms are compatible with tensor products, permutations, and fusion.

- **claim-borel-eigensheaf-existence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Assume <a href="#hyp:form" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:form">[hyp:form]</a>–<a href="#hyp:nilpotent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:nilpotent">[hyp:nilpotent]</a>. For every Zariski-dense parameter <a href="#eq:parameter" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameter">[eq:parameter]</a> satisfying <a href="#eq:regular-monodromy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:regular-monodromy">[eq:regular-monodromy]</a>, there is a nonzero locally constructible perverse geometric $E$-adic sheaf $M$ on $\mathcal A$ such that $$\relax(M)\subset\Lambda_{\mathrm{par}}.$$ For every finite set $I$ and every family $(V_i)_{i\in I}$, it carries isomorphisms $$\label{eq:main-eigen} \mathsf H_{I,(V_i)}(M) \simeq M\boxtimes\Bigl(\mathop{\boxtimes}_{i\in I}(V_i)_\sigma\Bigr) \quad\text{on }\mathcal A\times U^I,$$ natural in the representations and coherently compatible with the tensor unit, convolution, permutations of legs, and fusion along every collision diagonal.
  - Explanation: The prescribed dense geometric parameter admits a nonzero perverse eigensheaf at Borel level, with parabolic nilpotent singular support and the entire coherent moving-leg eigenstructure.
- **claim-geometric-nearby-cycle-formalism** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-nearby-foundations)): For the traits just specified, geometric nearby cycles preserve bounded constructibility and are exact for the perverse $t$-structures on the geometric fibers. They are unchanged by finite extension of the trait inside $\overline K$. The maps <a href="#eq:found-pull-exchange" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:found-pull-exchange">[eq:found-pull-exchange]</a>, <a href="#eq:found-push-exchange" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:found-push-exchange">[eq:found-push-exchange]</a>, and <a href="#eq:found-exterior-exchange" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:found-exterior-exchange">[eq:found-exterior-exchange]</a> are isomorphisms, respectively, for smooth $f$, for proper $f$, and for exterior products of bounded constructible complexes. These assertions extend to locally constructible complexes on the smooth stacks used here by smooth descent; proper morphisms in this extension are representable.* *Let $L_{\bar\eta}$ be a lisse finite-rank $E$-sheaf on a model’s geometric generic fiber. Suppose it has, after a finite extension of the coefficient field, a lisse lattice whose finite reductions extend to lisse sheaves after finite extensions of $S$. Suppose these extensions are compatible on further common extensions and have reductions of a lisse special-fiber sheaf $L_s$. The finite extension of $S$ may depend on the reduction. Then, for any map $g:Y\to Z$ to that model and any $F\in D^b_c(Y_{\bar\eta},E)$, the natural map is an isomorphism $$\label{eq:found-lisse-tensor} \Psi_Y F\otimes g_s^*L_s \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \Psi_Y(F\otimes g_{\bar\eta}^*L_{\bar\eta}).$$ The statement also holds locally on the stacks in question, and is compatible with tensor products and morphisms of the lisse systems.
  - Explanation: Geometric nearby cycles preserve constructibility and perversity. Smooth pullback, representable proper pushforward and exterior products have the stated exchange isomorphisms; tensoring with a specializing lisse system also commutes with nearby cycles.
- **claim-nilpotent-cone-dimension** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cone-dimension)): Let $\mathcal B$ be one of the bundle stacks just specified, and let $f:D\to\mathcal B$ be a smooth chart with $D$ a smooth finite-type scheme of pure dimension $d$. For the smooth cotangent pullback $\Lambda_D=f^\circ\Lambda\subset T^*D$, one has $$\label{eq:geometry-cone-dimension} \dim\Lambda_D\leq d.$$ In characteristic zero this cone is isotropic, in the sense that the symplectic form restricts to zero on the smooth locus of every reduced subvariety of $\Lambda_D$.
  - Explanation: The pulled-back nilpotent cone has dimension at most the dimension of the smooth chart. In characteristic zero, the asserted isotropy holds for every reduced subvariety.
- **claim-integral-central-cocharacter** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-central-cocharacter)): Over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, or over $k=\overline{\mathbb F}_q$ under the four characteristic hypotheses, let $a(t)\in\mathfrak g[[t]]$ with $a(0)$ not nilpotent. After changing the formal $G$-frame, there are a Levi subgroup $M$ and a cocharacter $\lambda:\mathbb G_m\to Z(M)$ such that $$\label{eq:geometry-levi-normal-form} \begin{gathered} a(t)\in\mathfrak m[[t]],\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{ad}}(a(0)):\mathfrak g/\mathfrak m\xrightarrow{\sim} \mathfrak g/\mathfrak m,\\ \kappa(a(0),d\lambda)\ne0. \end{gathered}$$ Here $d\lambda$ is the image of $1\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}\mathbb G_m$ under the differential of $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: A nonnilpotent constant Higgs value admits a formal Levi normal form and an actual central cocharacter with nonzero invariant pairing. The transverse adjoint operator is invertible.
- **claim-transverse-hecke-modification** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-transverse-hecke)): Let $A\in T^*_{b}\mathcal B$ be a covector outside $\Lambda$. There exist a point $y\in U$, an exact-relative-position correspondence $H$, a modification $h\in H$ with $p(h)=b$ and leg $y$, an output covector $A'\in T^*_{o(h)}\mathcal B$, and a nonzero $\xi\in T^*_yU$ such that $$\label{eq:geometry-covector-identity} p^*A=q^*(A',\xi)\quad\text{at }h.$$ Put $$H_{\mathrm{in}}=\widetilde p^{-1}(b,y),\qquad H_{\mathrm{out}}=q^{-1}(o(h),y),$$ where the fibers include the fixed-side bundle identifications. Both are smooth of dimension $m$. The following two sections have nondegenerate zeros at $h$:* 1. *on $H_{\mathrm{in}}$, the restriction of $p^*A$ to $T_{H/(\mathcal B\times U),q}\|_{H_{\mathrm{in}}}$;* 2. *on $H_{\mathrm{out}}$, the restriction of $o^*A'$ to $T_{H/(\mathcal B\times U),\widetilde p}\|_{H_{\mathrm{out}}}$.* *Each is a section of the dual of the indicated rank-$m$ bundle. In particular, both zeros are isolated.
  - Explanation: A covector outside the nilpotent cone admits a Hecke modification with a nonzero moving-leg component. The critical zeros on both fixed-side fibers are nondegenerate and isolated.
- **claim-nilpotent-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-nilpotent-detection)): Let $\mathcal B$ be such a bundle stack over an algebraically closed field, and let $F$ be a locally bounded constructible $E$-complex on $\mathcal B$. Suppose that, for every spherical Satake representation $V$ and each curve on which modifications are allowed, there is an isomorphism $$\mathsf H_V(F)\simeq F\boxtimes\mathcal V_V$$ with $\mathcal V_V$ lisse on the open curve. Then $\relax(b^*F)\subset\Lambda_D$ for every smooth finite-type scheme chart $b:D\to\mathcal B$.
  - Explanation: Lisse spherical Hecke eigenrelations force singular support into the generically nilpotent cone on every smooth chart.
- **claim-specialization-nonvanishing-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-specialization-detection)): In this relative setup, let $F$ be a locally bounded constructible $E$-complex on $\mathcal B_{\bar\eta}$ with lisse Hecke eigenvalues on $L_{\bar\eta}$. Suppose each eigenvalue admits a lisse lattice whose finite reductions extend lisse after finite extensions of the trait, compatibly with its special-fiber value, as in Proposition <a href="#prop:nearby-foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:nearby-foundations">2</a>. If $\Psi F=0$, then, on every smooth finite-type chart $D\to\mathcal B$, the closure of the nonzero-stalk locus of $F\|_{D_{\bar\eta}}$ does not meet $D_s$. Equivalently, any such meeting forces $\Psi F\ne0$.
  - Explanation: Under the relative Hecke geometry and compatible lattice specialization hypotheses, a generic nonzero-stalk locus approaching the special fiber forces nonzero geometric nearby cycles.
- **claim-hypersurface-cycle-vanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-detection-hypersurface)): If the differential $df_d$ in the field case, respectively its relative special-fiber differential in the trait case, lies outside $\Lambda_D$, then $$(\Phi_f K)_d=0 \quad\text{in the field case},\qquad \bigl(\Psi(K\|_{\{f=0\}_{\bar\eta}})\bigr)_d=0 \quad\text{in the trait case}.$$ Here $\Phi_f$ is the unshifted cone from restriction to geometric nearby cycles for $f$.
  - Explanation: A differential outside the nilpotent cone gives vanishing cycles equal to zero over a field, and gives zero nearby cycles for the hypersurface restriction in the trait argument.
- **claim-simultaneous-transverse-cuts** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-detection-cuts)): In the trait case, assume $\Psi F=0$. Let $f_1,\ldots,f_r$ vanish at $d\in D_s$ and suppose their special-fiber differentials span an $r$-dimensional subspace $V\subset T_d^*D_s$ with $V\cap(\Lambda_D)_d=\{0\}$. Then nearby cycles of $K\|_{\{f_1=\cdots=f_r=0\}_{\bar\eta}}$ vanish at $d$. The statement for $r=0$ is $\Psi K=0$.
  - Explanation: The trait vanishing assertion survives simultaneous cuts whose differentials span a subspace meeting the nilpotent cone only at the origin.
- **claim-coherent-hecke-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-hecke-specialization)): For $F\in D_{\mathrm{lcc}}(\mathcal B_{\bar\eta},E)$, any finite set $I$, and any representations $\boldsymbol V=(V_i)_{i\in I}$, there are natural isomorphisms $$\label{eq:spec-hecke-comparison} c_{I,\boldsymbol V}(F): \mathsf H^s_{I,\boldsymbol V}(\Psi_{\mathcal B}F) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \Psi_{\mathcal B\times_S U^I} \bigl(\mathsf H^{\bar\eta}_{I,\boldsymbol V}(F)\bigr).$$ They are compatible with the tensor unit, successive Hecke modifications and their convolution maps, permutations, and ordinary pullback along every collision diagonal. The functors have the relative normalization of <a href="#eq:found-hecke-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:found-hecke-definition">[eq:found-hecke-definition]</a>.* *Suppose, moreover, that $F$ has a coherent eigenstructure with lisse tensor eigenvalue $V\mapsto L_{\bar\eta,V}$ on $U_{\bar\eta}$. Suppose the lisse lattices specialize to a tensor system $V\mapsto L_{s,V}$ on $U_s$ as in Proposition <a href="#prop:nearby-foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:nearby-foundations">2</a>. Then $\Psi_{\mathcal B}F$ has a coherent eigenstructure with this special-fiber eigenvalue: $$\label{eq:spec-eigenvalue} \mathsf H^s_{I,\boldsymbol V}(\Psi F) \simeq (\Psi F)\boxtimes \boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{s,V_i}.$$ This conclusion does not assert that $\Psi F$ is nonzero; nonvanishing is supplied separately by Theorem <a href="#thm:specialization-detection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:specialization-detection">7</a>.
  - Explanation: Nearby cycles commute with the entire relative Hecke system, including successive modifications and collision diagonals. A specializing lisse tensor eigenvalue induces the specialized eigenstructure, with nonvanishing established separately.
- **claim-arbitrary-input-frame-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-spec-frame)): Let $\mathcal H\to\mathcal B\times_S U$ be a bounded one-step Hecke correspondence, viewed by its input-and-leg map, and let $K_{\bar\eta}$ be its relative Satake kernel. Let $b:B'\to\mathcal B\times_S U$ be any finite-type map of models and set $$W=B'\mathbin{\times}_{\mathcal B\times_S U}\mathcal H, \qquad r:W\to B',\qquad h:W\to\mathcal H.$$ For $D\in D^b_c(B'_{\bar\eta},E)$, the natural composite $$\begin{aligned} \Theta_b(D,K):\quad r_s^*\Psi_{B'}D\otimes h_s^*\Psi_{\mathcal H}K_{\bar\eta} &\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Ex}^*_r\otimes \operatorname{Ex}^*_h} \Psi_W(r_{\bar\eta}^*D)\otimes \Psi_W(h_{\bar\eta}^*K_{\bar\eta})\notag\\ &\xrightarrow{\mu} \Psi_W(r_{\bar\eta}^*D\otimes h_{\bar\eta}^*K_{\bar\eta}) \label{eq:spec-frame-map} \end{aligned}$$ is an isomorphism. The analogous statement for twisted products is obtained by frame descent. No smoothness assumption on $B'$ or on $b$ is required.
  - Explanation: The composite pull-and-tensor exchange is an isomorphism for a relative Satake kernel even when the input model is singular. Frame descent supplies the twisted-product version.
- **claim-satake-specialization-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-spec-satake)): Let $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\leq\lambda,S}$ be the split projective Schubert model for loops in the curve variable over $S$. Then $$\label{eq:spec-simple-satake} \Psi\mathop{\mathrm{IC}}_{\lambda,\bar\eta} \simeq\mathop{\mathrm{IC}}_{\lambda,s}.$$ Specialization is a symmetric tensor equivalence of the two spherical Satake categories and intertwines their cohomological fiber functors. Under Satake equivalence, it is tensor isomorphic to the identity on $\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}(\widehat G)$. Fix such a tensor identification in the rest of the paper.
  - Explanation: Specialization preserves simple spherical Satake objects and supplies a symmetric tensor equivalence preserving the cohomological fiber functors. A single tensor identification fixes the representation labels throughout.
- **claim-perverse-eigenstructure-preservation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-perverse-eigen)): Let $X/\mathbb C$ be a smooth projective connected curve, let $x\in X$, and put $U=X\setminus\{x\}$. Let $G$ be simple and simply connected, and let $\mathcal B$ be either of the two level stacks just defined. Suppose that a locally bounded constructible geometric $E$-adic complex $\mathcal F$ on $\mathcal B$ has a coherent Hecke eigenstructure for a Zariski-dense parameter $\sigma$ on $U$. For every $j\in\mathbb Z$, the perverse cohomology ${}^pH^j(\mathcal F)$ has an induced coherent eigenstructure with the same parameter and has singular support in $\Lambda$. This structure is natural in representations and compatible with the unit, convolution, permutations, and all collision diagonals in every $U^I$. If $\mathcal F\ne0$, at least one of these perverse eigensheaves is nonzero. No common bound on the cohomological degrees of $\mathcal F$ over $\mathcal B$ is required.
  - Explanation: For a dense parameter on the complex punctured curve, every perverse cohomology inherits the original coherent eigenstructure and nilpotent singular support. A nonzero locally bounded eigencomplex has a nonzero perverse eigenobject.
- **claim-spectral-center-scalar-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-perverse-scalar-center)): Let $K\in\mathscr D_\Lambda(\mathcal B)$ be a coherent Betti Hecke eigencomplex with parameter $\sigma$, represented by $y=(\rho(\gamma_1),\ldots,\rho(\gamma_a))\in Y(E)$. The degree-zero central endomorphism associated to $f\in E[Y]^{\widehat G}=\operatorname{End}^0_{\mathop{\mathrm{Perf}}(\mathscr L)}(\mathcal O)$ acts on $K$ as $f(y)\operatorname{id}_K$.
  - Explanation: An invariant spectral function acts on a coherent Betti eigencomplex by its value at the monodromy tuple. The proof evaluates coherent excursion maps rather than only object isomorphism classes.
- **claim-equivariant-frame-near-dense-orbit** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-perverse-frame)): Suppose that $y\in Y(E)$ generates a Zariski-dense subgroup of $\widehat G$. For every nonzero $V\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}(\widehat G)$ of dimension $b$ there are $f_V\in E[Y]^{\widehat G}$ with $f_V(y)\ne0$ and equivariant maps $$\alpha_V:\mathcal O_Y^{\oplus b}\longrightarrow\mathscr E_V, \qquad \beta_V:\mathscr E_V\longrightarrow\mathcal O_Y^{\oplus b}$$ such that $$\label{eq:perverse-adjugate} \beta_V\alpha_V=f_V\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal O_Y^{\oplus b}}, \qquad \alpha_V\beta_V=f_V\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr E_V}.$$
  - Explanation: Density gives a free closed orbit in the adjoint dual group. Equivariant sections extend from that orbit, and their invariant determinant and adjugate trivialize the representation bundle after localization.
- **claim-localized-hecke-exactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-perverse-localized-exactness)): Choose a frame as in Lemma <a href="#lem:perverse-frame" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:perverse-frame">15</a> for each isomorphism class of nonzero representations, and let $S$ be the multiplicative set generated by the $f_V$. The full subcategory $$\mathscr D_S= \{K\in\mathscr D^{\mathrm{lcc}}_\Lambda(\mathcal B): f_K:K\longrightarrow K\text{ is invertible for all }f\in S\}$$ is preserved by perverse truncations and by all fixed-point Hecke functors. Every fixed-point Hecke functor is perverse $t$-exact on $\mathscr D_S$.
  - Explanation: Inverting the invariant frame functions defines a category stable under perverse truncation and fixed-point Hecke operations. Those Hecke operations become direct sums of the identity and hence are perverse exact.
- **claim-nilpotent-support-implies-monodromicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-descent-monodromic)): Let $Q$ be a locally bounded constructible complex on $\mathcal A^+$ with singular support in the generic nilpotent cone. Its Betti realization is monodromic along $q$.
  - Explanation: Nilpotent singular support annihilates enhancement-fiber tangent directions, so the Betti realization is locally constant along the torsor factor while permitting arbitrary fiber monodromy.
- **claim-universal-monodromic-unit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-descent-unit)): Convolution with <a href="#eq:descent-unit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:descent-unit">[eq:descent-unit]</a> acts as the identity on complexes monodromic along a $T$-torsor. This identification is natural under base change, compatible with successive convolutions, and identifies the $R_T$-action with enhancement monodromy.
  - Explanation: The shifted regular local system acts as the convolution identity on torsor-monodromic complexes and identifies its coefficient-ring action with enhancement monodromy.
- **claim-radial-torsor-exchange** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-descent-radial)): Let $\Delta$ be an analytic disc, let $Y\to\Delta$ be a stratified analytic space, and factor a morphism over $\Delta$ as $$P\xrightarrow{\pi} C\xrightarrow{b}Y,$$ where $\pi$ is a $T$-torsor on the entire family and $b$ is proper. Work locally on finite-dimensional, paracompact charts. Let $F$ on $P\|_{\Delta^*}$ be weakly constructible and, in torsor charts, constructible for product stratifications with the $T$-factor. Then the exchange map $$\label{eq:descent-push-exchange} \Psi(b\pi)_*F\longrightarrow(b_0\pi_0)_*\Psi F$$ is an isomorphism. The same reduction gives the base-change and projection-formula isomorphisms for these direct images when the additional factors are pulled back from their targets. These identifications respect composition.
  - Explanation: Contracting the real radial factor reduces the relevant nonproper torus pushforward to a proper compact-torus pushforward. Nearby-cycle, base-change and projection-formula comparisons then hold for the stated product-constructible inputs.
- **claim-central-kernel-globalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-central-globalization)): Let $Q$ be a locally bounded constructible Betti complex on $\mathcal A^+$, monodromic along $q$, equipped with coherent Hecke eigenisomorphisms on $U$ with eigenvalue $\sigma$. The kernels $Z(V)$ act at $x$ on $Q$, by ordinary direct image in bounded affine Hecke correspondences, and there are isomorphisms $$\label{eq:descent-central-eigen} Z(V)\star_x Q\simeq Q\otimes_E V_{\sigma,\partial}.$$ They are natural in $V$, compatible with the tensor unit and successive convolutions, and carry $m_V\star_x\mathrm{id}_Q$ to $\mathrm{id}_Q\otimes M_{V,\partial}$. In a convolution of several central kernels this comparison holds for the monodromy on each individual factor. Consequently the action on $Q$ of <a href="#eq:descent-local-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:descent-local-trace">[eq:descent-local-trace]</a> is $$\label{eq:descent-global-trace} \chi_V(\text{enhancement monodromy}) =\operatorname{Tr}(M_{V,\partial})\,\mathrm{id}_Q.$$
  - Explanation: The local central kernels act globally on a torsor-monodromic eigencomplex. Their individual monodromies match the corresponding boundary eigenvalue monodromies, allowing the central trace to measure enhancement monodromy.
- **claim-unipotent-enhancement-descent** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-torus-descent)): Let $\sigma$ be a Zariski-dense geometric adic parameter on $U$ whose local monodromy at $x$ is unipotent. If $\mathcal A^+$ carries a nonzero locally bounded constructible geometric adic Hecke eigencomplex for $\sigma$, with the coherent tensor and fusion data, then $\mathcal A$ carries a nonzero locally constructible perverse geometric adic Hecke eigensheaf for the same parameter and with the same coherences. Its singular support lies in the parabolic global nilpotent cone.
  - Explanation: A dense eigencomplex with unipotent boundary monodromy descends from enhanced flags to a nonzero ordinary-flag perverse eigensheaf. Central traces force enhancement monodromy to be unipotent, ensuring nonzero compact-support pushforward.
- **claim-compact-unramified-eigencomplex** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-compact-eigencomplex)): Let $C$ be a smooth projective connected curve over an algebraically closed field $K$ of characteristic zero, and let $G$ be simple and simply connected. Let $\tau$ be a continuous $\widehat G$-local system on $C$, defined over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$, with Zariski-dense image. There is a nonzero locally bounded constructible geometric $E$-adic complex $F$ on $\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(C)$ with a coherent Hecke eigenstructure of eigenvalue $\tau$. The eigenstructure uses the relative normalization of Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">2</a> and includes all finite sets of legs and their collision maps.
  - Explanation: The characteristic-zero unramified correspondence transports the compact spectral skyscraper of a dense parameter to a nonzero locally bounded constructible eigencomplex with the full coherent Hecke system.
- **claim-prescribed-node-type-flags** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-construction-type-flags)): With these choices there is an equivalence $$\label{eq:construction-special-bun} (\mathcal B_n)_0 \simeq [\mathcal A_1^+\times\mathcal A_2^+/T],$$ where $T$ changes the two identifications at the gluing gerbe simultaneously. Hecke modifications at an unmarked point of $X_i$ pull back to the usual Hecke modifications on the $i$th factor.
  - Explanation: The prescribed alcove stabilizer type identifies the nodal special-fiber bundle stack with the simultaneous torus quotient of enhanced flags on the normalized components. Unmarked Hecke modifications act on their respective factors.
- **claim-characteristic-zero-borel-existence** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-charzero-existence)): Let $(X,x)$ be a smooth projective pointed complex curve of genus at least two, let $G$ be simple and simply connected, and let $\sigma$ be a continuous Zariski-dense geometric $E$-adic $\widehat G$-parameter on $X\setminus\{x\}$, defined over a finite coefficient extension. If its local monodromy is unipotent, there is a nonzero locally constructible perverse eigensheaf on $\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_{G,B,x}(X)$ with eigenvalue $\sigma$, nilpotent singular support, and the full coherent Hecke system in our relative normalization.
  - Explanation: The separating-node construction and enhancement descent produce a nonzero perverse Borel-level eigensheaf on the complex curve for a dense parameter with unipotent local monodromy.
- **claim-mixed-characteristic-parameter-lift** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-construction-mixed-parameter)): The given parameter $\sigma$ lifts to a continuous parameter $\sigma_{K_0}$ on $\mathscr U_{K_0}$ with the same compact image and the same full boundary inertia homomorphism, after the compatible identification of prime-to-$p$ roots of unity. In particular it is Zariski dense, its local inertia factors through $\mathbb Z_\ell(1)$ and is regular unipotent, and its lattice reductions specialize to those of $\sigma$ on $U$.
  - Explanation: Compatible root-stack torsors lift the parameter to the geometric characteristic-zero fiber while preserving its compact image, full boundary inertia and regular-unipotent condition. Their lattice reductions specialize to the original tensor local system.

          "exact_quote": "Let $S=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$ be an excellent complete strictly henselian discrete valuation trait, with algebraically closed residue field $k$, and with $\\ell$ invertible in $R$.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.6"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:found-lisse-tensor",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L_{\\bar\\eta}$ be a lisse finite-rank $E$-sheaf on a model’s geometric generic fiber.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.6"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:found-lisse-tensor",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose it has, after a finite extension of the coefficient field, a lisse lattice whose finite reductions extend to lisse sheaves after finite extensions of $S$.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.6"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:found-lisse-tensor",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose these extensions are compatible on further common extensions and have reductions of a lisse special-fiber sheaf $L_s$.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.6"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-geometric-nearby-cycle-formalism",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:found-lisse-tensor",
          "exact_quote": "Then, for any map $g:Y\\to Z$ to that model and any $F\\in D^b_c(Y_{\\bar\\eta},E)$, the natural map is an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:found-lisse-tensor}\n  \\Psi_Y F\\otimes g_s^*L_s\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n             \\Psi_Y(F\\otimes g_{\\bar\\eta}^*L_{\\bar\\eta}).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-014.6"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-nearby-foundations"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-cone-dimension",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal B$ be one of the bundle stacks just specified, and let $f:D\\to\\mathcal B$ be a smooth chart with $D$ a smooth finite-type scheme of pure dimension $d$.         "source_id": "PM-014.6"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-central-cocharacter",
        "exact_quote": "Over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, or over $k=\\overline{\\mathbb F}_q$ under the four characteristic hypotheses, let $a(t)\\in\\mathfrak g[[t]]$ with $a(0)$ not nilpotent.       },

Both are smooth of dimension $m$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-hecke-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "For $F\\in D_{\\mathrm{lcc}}(\\mathcal B_{\\bar\\eta},E)$, any finite set $I$, and any representations $\\boldsymbol V=(V_i)_{i\\in I}$, there are natural isomorphisms $$\\label{eq:spec-hecke-comparison}\n c_{I,\\boldsymbol V}(F):\n \\mathsf H^s_{I,\\boldsymbol V}(\\Psi_{\\mathcal B}F)\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n \\Psi_{\\mathcal B\\times_S U^I}\n                      \\bigl(\\mathsf H^{\\bar\\eta}_{I,\\boldsymbol V}(F)\\bigr).$$ They are compatible with the tensor unit, successive Hecke modifications and their convolution maps, permutations, and ordinary pullback along every collision diagonal. The functors have the relative normalization of <a href=\"#eq:found-hecke-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:found-hecke-definition\">[eq:found-hecke-definition]</a>.*\n\n\n*Suppose, moreover, that $F$ has a coherent eigenstructure with lisse tensor eigenvalue $V\\mapsto L_{\\bar\\eta,V}$ on $U_{\\bar\\eta}$. Then $\\Psi_{\\mathcal B}F$ has a coherent eigenstructure with this special-fiber eigenvalue: $$\\label{eq:spec-eigenvalue}\n \\mathsf H^s_{I,\\boldsymbol V}(\\Psi F)\n       \\simeq (\\Psi F)\\boxtimes\n                         \\boxtimes_{i\\in I}L_{s,V_i}.$$ This conclusion does not assert that $\\Psi F$ is nonzero; nonvanishing is supplied separately by Theorem <a href=\"#thm:specialization-detection\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:specialization-detection\">7</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.6"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-spec-frame",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal H\\to\\mathcal B\\times_S U$ be a bounded one-step Hecke correspondence, viewed by its input-and-leg map, and let $K_{\\bar\\eta}$ be its relative Satake kernel. Let $b:B'\\to\\mathcal B\\times_S U$ be any finite-type map of models and set $$W=B'\\mathbin{\\times}_{\\mathcal B\\times_S U}\\mathcal H,\n \\qquad r:W\\to B',\\qquad h:W\\to\\mathcal H.$$ For $D\\in D^b_c(B'_{\\bar\\eta},E)$, the natural composite $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Theta_b(D,K):\\quad\n r_s^*\\Psi_{B'}D\\otimes h_s^*\\Psi_{\\mathcal H}K_{\\bar\\eta}\n &\\xrightarrow{\\operatorname{Ex}^*_r\\otimes\n                             \\operatorname{Ex}^*_h}\n \\Psi_W(r_{\\bar\\eta}^*D)\\otimes\n                      \\Psi_W(h_{\\bar\\eta}^*K_{\\bar\\eta})\\notag\\\\\n &\\xrightarrow{\\mu}\n \\Psi_W(r_{\\bar\\eta}^*D\\otimes\n                            h_{\\bar\\eta}^*K_{\\bar\\eta})\n                       \\label{eq:spec-frame-map}\n\\end{aligned}$$ is an isomorphism. The analogous statement for twisted products is obtained by frame descent. No smoothness assumption on $B'$ or on $b$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-spec-frame",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal H\\to\\mathcal B\\times_S U$ be a bounded one-step Hecke correspondence, viewed by its input-and-leg map, and let $K_{\\bar\\eta}$ be its relative Satake kernel.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-spec-satake",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}_{\\leq\\lambda,S}$ be the split projective Schubert model for loops in the curve variable over $S$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-perverse-eigen",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X/\\mathbb C$ be a smooth projective connected curve, let $x\\in X$, and put $U=X\\setminus\\{x\\}$. Let $G$ be simple and simply connected, and let $\\mathcal B$ be either of the two level stacks just defined. Suppose that a locally bounded constructible geometric $E$-adic complex $\\mathcal F$ on $\\mathcal B$ has a coherent Hecke eigenstructure for a Zariski-dense parameter $\\sigma$ on $U$. For every $j\\in\\mathbb Z$, the perverse cohomology ${}^pH^j(\\mathcal F)$ has an induced coherent eigenstructure with the same parameter and has singular support in $\\Lambda$. This structure is natural in representations and compatible with the unit, convolution, permutations, and all collision diagonals in every $U^I$. If $\\mathcal F\\ne0$, at least one of these perverse eigensheaves is nonzero. No common bound on the cohomological degrees of $\\mathcal F$ over $\\mathcal B$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.6"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-perverse-frame",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $y\\in Y(E)$ generates a Zariski-dense subgroup of $\\widehat G$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-perverse-localized-exactness",
        "exact_quote": "Choose a frame as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:perverse-frame\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:perverse-frame\">15</a> for each isomorphism class of nonzero representations, and let $S$ be the multiplicative set generated by the $f_V$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-descent-radial",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Delta$ be an analytic disc, let $Y\\to\\Delta$ be a stratified analytic space, and factor a morphism over $\\Delta$ as $$P\\xrightarrow{\\pi} C\\xrightarrow{b}Y,$$ where $\\pi$ is a $T$-torsor on the entire family and $b$ is proper. Work locally on finite-dimensional, paracompact charts. Let $F$ on $P|_{\\Delta^*}$ be weakly constructible and, in torsor charts, constructible for product stratifications with the $T$-factor. Then the exchange map $$\\label{eq:descent-push-exchange}\n \\Psi(b\\pi)_*F\\longrightarrow(b_0\\pi_0)_*\\Psi F$$ is an isomorphism. The same reduction gives the base-change and projection-formula isomorphisms for these direct images when the additional factors are pulled back from their targets. These identifications respect composition.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-descent-radial",

Then the exchange map $$\\label{eq:descent-push-exchange}\n \\Psi(b\\pi)_*F\\longrightarrow(b_0\\pi_0)_*\\Psi F$$ is an isomorphism. The same reduction gives the base-change and projection-formula isomorphisms for these direct images when the additional factors are pulled back from their targets. These identifications respect composition.",

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-central-globalization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Q$ be a locally bounded constructible Betti complex on $\\mathcal A^+$, monodromic along $q$, equipped with coherent Hecke eigenisomorphisms on $U$ with eigenvalue $\\sigma$. The kernels $Z(V)$ act at $x$ on $Q$, by ordinary direct image in bounded affine Hecke correspondences, and there are isomorphisms $$\\label{eq:descent-central-eigen}\n Z(V)\\star_x Q\\simeq Q\\otimes_E V_{\\sigma,\\partial}.$$ They are natural in $V$, compatible with the tensor unit and successive convolutions, and carry $m_V\\star_x\\mathrm{id}_Q$ to $\\mathrm{id}_Q\\otimes M_{V,\\partial}$. In a convolution of several central kernels this comparison holds for the monodromy on each individual factor. Consequently the action on $Q$ of <a href=\"#eq:descent-local-trace\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:descent-local-trace\">[eq:descent-local-trace]</a> is $$\\label{eq:descent-global-trace}\n \\chi_V(\\text{enhancement monodromy})\n =\\operatorname{Tr}(M_{V,\\partial})\\,\\mathrm{id}_Q.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-014.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-central-globalization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Q$ be a locally bounded constructible Betti complex on $\\mathcal A^+$, monodromic along $q$, equipped with coherent Hecke eigenisomorphisms on $U$ with eigenvalue $\\sigma$. The kernels $Z(V)$ act at $x$ on $Q$, by ordinary direct image in bounded affine Hecke correspondences, and there are isomorphisms $$\\label{eq:descent-central-eigen}\n Z(V)\\star_x Q\\simeq Q\\otimes_E V_{\\sigma,\\partial}.$$ They are natural in $V$, compatible with the tensor unit and successive convolutions, and carry $m_V\\star_x\\mathrm{id}_Q$ to $\\mathrm{id}_Q\\otimes M_{V,\\partial}$. In a convolution of several central kernels this comparison holds for the monodromy on each individual factor. Consequently the action on $Q$ of <a href=\"#eq:descent-local-trace\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:descent-local-trace\">[eq:descent-local-trace]</a> is $$\\label{eq:descent-global-trace}\n \\chi_V(\\text{enhancement monodromy})\n =\\operatorname{Tr}(M_{V,\\partial})\\,\\mathrm{id}_Q.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-014.6"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $Q$ be a locally bounded constructible Betti complex on $\\mathcal A^+$, monodromic along $q$, equipped with coherent Hecke eigenisomorphisms on $U$ with eigenvalue $\\sigma$. In a convolution of several central kernels this comparison holds for the monodromy on each individual factor. Consequently the action on $Q$ of <a href=\"#eq:descent-local-trace\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:descent-local-trace\">[eq:descent-local-trace]</a> is $$\\label{eq:descent-global-trace}\n \\chi_V(\\text{enhancement monodromy})\n =\\operatorname{Tr}(M_{V,\\partial})\\,\\mathrm{id}_Q.$$",
      "explanation": "The local central kernels act globally on a torsor-monodromic eigencomplex. Their individual monodromies match the corresponding boundary eigenvalue monodromies, allowing the central trace to measure enhancement monodromy.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-central-globalization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Q$ be a locally bounded constructible Betti complex on $\\mathcal A^+$, monodromic along $q$, equipped with coherent Hecke eigenisomorphisms on $U$ with eigenvalue $\\sigma$.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.6"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-central-kernel-globalization",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-central-globalization"

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-torus-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\sigma$ be a Zariski-dense geometric adic parameter on $U$ whose local monodromy at $x$ is unipotent. If $\\mathcal A^+$ carries a nonzero locally bounded constructible geometric adic Hecke eigencomplex for $\\sigma$, with the coherent tensor and fusion data, then $\\mathcal A$ carries a nonzero locally constructible perverse geometric adic Hecke eigensheaf for the same parameter and with the same coherences. Its singular support lies in the parabolic global nilpotent cone.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.6"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-eigencomplex",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective connected curve over an algebraically closed field $K$ of characteristic zero, and let $G$ be simple and simply connected. Let $\\tau$ be a continuous $\\widehat G$-local system on $C$, defined over a finite extension of $\\mathbb Q_\\ell$, with Zariski-dense image. There is a nonzero locally bounded constructible geometric $E$-adic complex $F$ on $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bun}}_G(C)$ with a coherent Hecke eigenstructure of eigenvalue $\\tau$. The eigenstructure uses the relative normalization of Section <a href=\"#sec:foundations\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:foundations\">2</a> and includes all finite sets of legs and their collision maps.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-charzero-existence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,x)$ be a smooth projective pointed complex curve of genus at least two, let $G$ be simple and simply connected, and let $\\sigma$ be a continuous Zariski-dense geometric $E$-adic $\\widehat G$-parameter on $X\\setminus\\{x\\}$, defined over a finite coefficient extension. In particular it is Zariski dense, its local inertia factors through $\\mathbb Z_\\ell(1)$ and is regular unipotent, and its lattice reductions specialize to those of $\\sigma$ on $U$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **geometric adic parameter** ([source](#eq:parameter)): A parameter $\sigma$ is a continuous homomorphism $$\label{eq:parameter} \rho:\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(U,\bar u)\longrightarrow\widehat G(L),$$ up to $\widehat G(E)$-conjugacy, where $L/\mathbb Q_\ell$ is finite inside $E$.
- **regular-unipotent boundary condition** ([source](#eq:regular-monodromy)): The regular-unipotent condition is $$\label{eq:regular-monodromy} \rho(\gamma)=\exp\bigl(t_\ell(\gamma)N\bigr),\qquad \gamma\in I_x, \qquad \dim Z_{\widehat G}(N)=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(\widehat G),$$ for a nilpotent $N\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(\widehat G)(L)$ after a finite extension of $L$ and a conjugation.
- **Borel-level bundle stack** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $$\mathcal A=\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_{G,B,x}(X)$$ be the stack of $G$-bundles $P$ on $X$ with a $B$-reduction $\beta$ at $x$.
- **locally constructible perverse sheaf** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A geometric $E$-adic sheaf $M$ on $\mathcal A$ is *locally constructible perverse* if, for every smooth map $f:S\to\mathcal A$ from a smooth finite-type $k$-scheme of constant relative dimension $d$, the complex $f^*M[d]$ is bounded constructible and perverse.
- **parabolic global nilpotent cone** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The *parabolic global nilpotent cone* $\Lambda_{\mathrm{par}}$ consists of these triples for which $\phi$ is generically nilpotent.
- **locally bounded constructible category** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a smooth algebraic stack $\mathcal B$ locally of finite type over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $\ell$, let $D_{\mathrm{lcc}}(\mathcal B,E)$ denote the category of geometric adic complexes whose pullback to every smooth finite-type scheme chart is bounded and constructible.
- **smooth cotangent pullback** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a closed conic subset $C\subset T^*\mathcal B$, its pullback to a smooth chart is $$f^{\circ}C =\operatorname{image}\bigl(D\mathbin{\times}_{\mathcal B}C \longrightarrow T^*D\bigr).$$
- **enhanced flag** ([source](#eq:found-node-quotient)): An enhanced flag is an $N_B$-reduction of the fiber at $x$; forgetting the enhancement gives a representable $T$-torsor $$\label{eq:found-level-torsor} \pi:\mathcal A^+\longrightarrow\mathcal A.$$
- **geometric nearby cycles** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a finite-type $S$-scheme $Y$ we use geometric nearby cycles $$\Psi_Y:D^b_c(Y_{\bar\eta},E) \longrightarrow D^b_c(Y_s,E).$$
- **relative Hecke functor** ([source](#eq:found-hecke-definition)): Define $$\label{eq:found-hecke-definition} \mathsf H_{I,\boldsymbol V}(F) =o_{I,*}\bigl(F\,\widetilde\boxtimes\, \operatorname{Sat}_I(\boldsymbol V)\bigr).$$
- **coherent Hecke eigenstructure** ([source](#eq:found-fusion)): An eigenstructure throughout this paper means natural isomorphisms for all $I$ and all representations, compatible with these fusion maps, the unit, successive modifications, and permutations.
- **torsor monodromicity** ([source](#pm-h-26)): We call a complex on a $T$-torsor *monodromic along the torsor* if, locally on its base, it is constructible for a product stratification $\{S_\alpha\times T\}$ and its restriction to a contractible patch in the $T$-factor is pulled back from a slice.
- **universal monodromic convolution unit** ([source](#eq:descent-unit)): We use ordinary direct image in the convolution of such kernels, and its unit is $$\label{eq:descent-unit} \mathbf 1_T=\mathscr L_T[r].$$

| q-chart-nilpotent-cone-dimension | \dim\Lambda_D | ≤ | $d$ | The chart dimension and its smooth cotangent pullback cone. |

| q-punctured-surface-generator-count | a | = | $2g(X)$ | The chosen free monodromy generators. |
| q-positive-alcove-root-weight | \langle\alpha,\lambda\rangle | > | $0$ | Every positive root and the chosen strictly dominant cocharacter. |
| q-alcove-root-weight-ceiling | \langle\alpha,\lambda\rangle | < | $n$ | Every positive root; the root index is chosen sufficiently large for the fixed cocharacter. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-nearby-foundations** [claim](#stmt-prop-nearby-foundations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nearby-foundations): Geometric coefficient descent and universally locally acyclic extension establish constructibility, perverse exactness and the permitted exchanges. -> The geometric nearby-cycle formalism used by both specializations.
- **step-cone-dimension** [claim](#stmt-prop-cone-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cone-dimension): Lift a generic nilpotent Higgs field to a parabolic reduction after a separable extension and show that the canonical cotangent form vanishes there. -> The chart cone dimension bound and characteristic-zero isotropy.
- **step-central-cocharacter** [claim](#stmt-lem-central-cocharacter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-central-cocharacter): Separate the semisimple part, identify its Levi centralizer, span the Lie center by integral cocharacter differentials and eliminate off-Levi coefficients recursively. -> A Levi normal form with a nonzero central-cocharacter pairing.
- **step-transverse-modification** [claim](#stmt-prop-transverse-hecke) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transverse-hecke): Modify by the central cocharacter and compute both tangent lattices, their perfect residue pairing and its adjoint twist. -> A nonzero leg covector and isolated nondegenerate zeros on both fixed-side fibers.
- **step-hypersurface-detection** [claim](#stmt-lem-detection-hypersurface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detection-hypersurface): Use the eigenrelation in a double Hecke correspondence, isolate a stalk in proper pushforward, obtain an exact split quadratic equation and recover the original cut through a projective quadric triangle. -> Vanishing of the cycles associated with a differential outside the cone.
- **step-nilpotent-detection** [claim](#stmt-thm-nilpotent-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detection-hypersurface-2): Apply the hypersurface test on all charts and identify weak singular support with full geometric adic singular support. -> Nilpotent singular support for every permitted lisse Hecke eigencomplex.
- **step-simultaneous-cuts** [claim](#stmt-lem-detection-cuts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detection-cuts): Replace successive cuts by a proper projective incidence family and use its top-cohomology quotient to recover the simultaneous intersection. -> Nearby-cycle vanishing on the simultaneous cut.
- **step-specialization-detection** [claim](#stmt-thm-specialization-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detection-cuts-2): Choose a general support point and transverse cuts using the cone bound, retain a nonzero generic stalk and reduce its support to a finite local trait piece. -> A contradiction to zero nearby cycles, establishing specialization nonvanishing.
- **step-arbitrary-input-frame** [claim](#stmt-lem-spec-frame) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spec-frame): In smooth coordinate-and-frame covers, identify the correspondence with a product of the arbitrary input and a fixed Schubert model, then apply exterior-product exchange. -> The composite frame exchange isomorphism without input smoothness.
- **step-satake-specialization** [claim](#stmt-lem-spec-satake) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spec-satake): Use perverse exactness, Satake semisimplicity and proper cohomology comparison to exclude extra constituents; compare convolution and fusion through the frame calculation. -> A symmetric tensor specialization equivalence with a fixed representation-label identification.
- **step-coherent-hecke-specialization** [claim](#stmt-prop-hecke-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spec-satake-2): Iterate the frame comparison through successive modifications, use proper convolution and prove diagonal exchange by induction before transporting every coherence diagram. -> The full specialized Hecke eigenstructure including collisions.
- **step-scalar-spectral-center** [claim](#stmt-lem-perverse-scalar-center) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-perverse-scalar-center): Express invariant functions through representation traces and evaluate the corresponding coherent excursion operations on the eigencomplex. -> Scalar action of invariant spectral functions at the parameter.
- **step-dense-orbit-frame** [claim](#stmt-lem-perverse-frame) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-perverse-frame): Use the free closed dense orbit, exactness of invariants, extended equivariant sections and the determinant-adjugate identities. -> An invariant function nonzero at the parameter and equivariant inverse frames after localization.
- **step-localized-exactness** [claim](#stmt-lem-perverse-localized-exactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-perverse-localized-exactness): Commute central endomorphisms with truncation and apply the frame maps to identify fixed-point Hecke functors with copies of the identity. -> A truncation-stable category with perverse-exact fixed-point Hecke operations.
- **step-perverse-coherent-eigenmaps** [claim](#stmt-prop-perverse-eigen) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-perverse-localized-exactness-2): Place the eigencomplex in the localized category by scalar evaluation, extend exactness by full-product leg transport and truncate the original coherent maps with the explicit leg shifts. -> Coherent geometric adic eigenstructures on perverse cohomologies, including a nonzero one when the original complex is nonzero.
- **step-enhancement-monodromicity** [claim](#stmt-lem-descent-monodromic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-descent-monodromic): Use zero toral residues and a product microlocal stratification to obtain locally constant continuation along the enhancement fibers. -> Torsor monodromicity of the Betti realization.
- **step-monodromic-unit** [claim](#stmt-lem-descent-unit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-descent-unit): Compute the regular-local-system convolution through the Koszul complex, or through oriented compact-support integration on the real cover. -> The natural convolution-unit identity and the enhancement monodromy action.
- **step-radial-exchange** [claim](#stmt-lem-descent-radial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-descent-radial): Remove the contractible radial subgroup across the entire family and apply proper base change to the remaining compact-torus bundle. -> Nearby-cycle and direct-image exchange with composition, base-change and projection-formula compatibility.
- **step-central-globalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-central-globalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-central-globalization): Retain independent enhancements during collision, specialize the moving action through radial exchange and compare successive actions while preserving each factor monodromy. -> The global central action and the enhancement-to-boundary trace identity.
- **step-torus-descent** [claim](#stmt-prop-torus-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-torus-descent): Use central characters to force unipotent enhancement monodromy, show top compact-support cohomology has nonzero coinvariants and transport the eigenmaps through torsor pushforward and perverse cohomology. -> A nonzero ordinary-flag perverse eigensheaf with nilpotent support and full coherence.
- **step-compact-unramified-object** [claim](#stmt-lem-compact-eigencomplex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compact-eigencomplex): Apply the unramified characteristic-zero equivalence to the compact spectral skyscraper of the dense parameter and transport universal tensor evaluation. -> A nonzero locally bounded constructible coherent unramified eigencomplex.
- **step-nodal-type-identification** [claim](#stmt-lem-construction-type-flags) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-construction-type-flags): Compute equivariant root-disc frame changes for the prescribed alcove type, identify ordinary and enhanced levels on each branch and glue their gerbe frames modulo the simultaneous torus. -> The special-fiber simultaneous torus quotient and its factorwise Hecke action.
- **step-characteristic-zero-existence** [claim](#stmt-prop-charzero-existence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-charzero-existence): Glue compatible finite parameter quotients through inverse boundary monodromies, lift their tower, specialize the compact eigencomplex at the prescribed type, restrict to an enhanced factor and descend its torus enhancement. -> The characteristic-zero ordinary Borel-level perverse eigensheaf.
- **step-mixed-parameter-lift** [claim](#stmt-lem-construction-mixed-parameter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-construction-mixed-parameter): Lift the compatible finite torsors to tame relative root stacks through proper henselian invariance and retain their full inertia actions and tensor reductions. -> A dense characteristic-zero parameter with the same full boundary homomorphism and specializing lattices.
- **step-main-existence** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-construction-mixed-parameter-2): Construct on the geometric characteristic-zero fiber, take geometric nearby cycles, extend a nonzero generic stalk through a bounded Hecke modification and a proper flag bundle, then apply specialization and nilpotent detection. -> The nonzero positive-characteristic perverse eigensheaf of the main theorem with parabolic nilpotent support and all coherences.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-geometric-eigensheaf-existence**: Under the stated characteristic, curve, group, and parameter hypotheses, a nonzero locally constructible perverse geometric eigensheaf exists at ordinary Borel level, with parabolic nilpotent singular support and the full coherent Hecke system.
- **invariant-support-meeting-forces-specialization**: For a locally bounded constructible complex with compatible lisse Hecke eigenvalues in the specified relative setup, a meeting of the generic nonzero-stalk closure with the special fiber forces nonzero geometric nearby cycles.
- **invariant-perverse-cohomology-preserves-coherence**: Perverse cohomology of a locally bounded constructible coherent eigencomplex with dense parameter retains its eigenvalue, nilpotent singular support, and all Hecke coherences. A nonzero input has a nonzero perverse eigenobject.
- **invariant-unipotent-enhancement-descent**: A nonzero coherent enhanced-level eigencomplex for a dense parameter with unipotent boundary monodromy yields a nonzero ordinary-level perverse eigensheaf with the same parameter and coherences.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-arithmetic-frobenius-output**: Treat the geometric existence theorem as producing Frobenius descent or arithmetic trace data. -> The parameter and the constructed sheaf are geometric; the theorem supplies no Frobenius structure.
- **anti-automatic-nearby-cycle-nonvanishing**: Infer that nearby cycles of any nonzero generic eigencomplex are nonzero without checking its support closure. -> Hecke specialization preserves the eigenstructure but does not establish nonvanishing; the detection argument requires a meeting with the special fiber.
- **anti-unrestricted-hecke-exactness**: Apply perverse truncation to arbitrary Hecke transforms and assume that the original eigenmaps survive. -> The proof establishes fixed-point exactness on the localized nilpotent subcategory associated with the dense parameter, rather than on arbitrary complexes.
- **anti-arbitrary-torus-monodromy-descent**: Push an arbitrary nonzero enhanced-flag complex to ordinary flags and assume that its direct image remains nonzero. -> The direct image can vanish for arbitrary fiber monodromy; the argument derives unipotent enhancement monodromy from the coherent eigenstructure and unipotent boundary monodromy.
- **anti-general-pull-exchange**: Treat pull exchange or tensor exchange for nearby cycles as an isomorphism for an arbitrary morphism or arbitrary tensor factors. -> The general exchange maps do not carry that guarantee; the frame lemma proves a specific composite comparison using local exterior products.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The result constructs geometric sheaf objects through categorical equivalence, geometric nearby cycles, perverse cohomology, and torus descent. It does not specify a finite computational representation of these objects or a terminating procedure deciding their eigenstructure. The source explicitly states: “This condition is local on the bundle stack; it requires no bound on the Harder–Narasimhan strata supporting $M$.” It also states: “No common bound on the cohomological degrees of $\mathcal F$ over $\mathcal B$ is required.” Finite checks of auxiliary matrices or cohomology dimensions would therefore leave the global existence, nonvanishing, singular-support, and coherence assertions undecided.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-main-curve-and-group-setting** (hypothesis): The positive-characteristic existence theorem uses the specified smooth projective pointed curve and a split, simple, simply connected group of positive rank. Its existence conclusion cannot be transferred directly to other curve or group settings. Blocked use: ['Apply the main existence theorem to curves outside its genus hypothesis.', 'Assert the same Borel-level existence result for arbitrary reductive groups.']
- **limit-invariant-form-on-levi-centers** (hypothesis): The invariant form must remain nondegenerate on every Levi Lie-algebra center. Nondegeneracy on the full Lie algebra alone does not establish this hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Perform the central-cocharacter pairing argument with a form that degenerates on a Levi center.']
- **limit-chevalley-restriction** (hypothesis): The invariant-polynomial description of generic nilpotence requires the stated Chevalley restriction hypothesis for every Levi. Blocked use: ['Identify the invariant zero fiber with the nilpotent cone without establishing the restriction isomorphisms.']
- **limit-scheme-theoretic-semismple-centralizers** (hypothesis): The centralizer hypothesis concerns scheme-theoretic centralizers of semisimple Lie-algebra elements. A statement only about centralizer points does not establish the required Levi property. Blocked use: ['Use an unverified or non-Levi centralizer in the formal normal-form construction.']
- **limit-rational-nilpotent-parabolics** (hypothesis): Nilpotent elements must belong to a parabolic nilradical over each field extension in which the argument is performed. Geometric containment after an unspecified extension is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Construct the generic parabolic reduction using only algebraically closed-field nilpotent-orbit information.']
- **limit-dense-tame-regular-parameter** (hypothesis): The main theorem requires a continuous dense geometric parameter with the prescribed tame regular-unipotent boundary condition. The characteristic-zero existence and torus-descent propositions require unipotence without the additional regularity condition. Blocked use: ['Apply the main theorem directly to a parameter with wild inertia.', 'Discard density in the spectral-frame and perverse-cohomology arguments.', 'Treat regularity as a necessary hypothesis of the torus-descent proposition.']
- **limit-geometric-not-arithmetic-descent** (formal_scope): The parameter and output sheaves are geometric. Frobenius descent and arithmetic trace data are not supplied. Blocked use: ['Extract Frobenius traces from the existence result without a separate descent theorem.']
- **limit-chart-local-bounds** (formal_scope): Local bounded constructibility is tested on smooth finite-type charts. It neither asserts local constancy nor provides a global cohomological or Harder–Narasimhan support bound. Blocked use: ['Replace locally bounded constructible input by a locally constant sheaf assumption.', 'Assume that the constructed eigensheaf has support in a fixed finite-type substack.', 'Require a common global cohomological bound as a consequence of perversity.']
- **limit-permitted-nearby-cycle-exchanges** (hypothesis): General pull and tensor exchange maps are not asserted to be isomorphisms. The permitted exchanges require their specific smoothness, representable properness, exterior-product, lisse-system, or frame-comparison hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Commute nearby cycles with an arbitrary pullback or arbitrary nonproper pushforward.', "Conclude that the individual pull-exchange arrows are isomorphisms from the frame lemma's composite comparison."]
- **limit-reduction-dependent-trait-extensions** (formal_scope): Compatible finite lattice reductions may require different finite trait extensions. The formalism does not require or produce a common finite extension supporting the entire adic system. Blocked use: ['Replace the compatible reduction tower by an asserted common finite descent field.']
- **limit-specialization-needs-support-meeting** (hypothesis): Preservation of a coherent eigenstructure under specialization does not itself prove nonvanishing. The detection theorem requires the generic nonzero-stalk closure to meet a relevant special-fiber chart; the nodal construction must establish that meeting at the prescribed type. Blocked use: ['Infer nonzero nearby cycles solely from a nonzero generic eigencomplex.', 'Substitute nonvanishing on an unspecified nodal component for nonvanishing at the prescribed stabilizer type.']
- **limit-full-moving-leg-coherence** (formal_scope): An eigenstructure includes the unit, successive modifications, permutations, and fusion on collision diagonals. Separate fixed-point eigenisomorphisms do not supply this output. Blocked use: ['Treat independently chosen fixed-point eigenmaps as a coherent eigenstructure.', 'Ignore collisions when specializing or truncating the Hecke system.', 'Insert an unproved moving-leg or codimension shift into the relative-normalized eigenrelation.']
- **limit-localized-perverse-exactness** (hypothesis): Fixed-point Hecke exactness is established on the localized nilpotent subcategory where the invariant frame functions act invertibly. Moving-leg truncation additionally uses local constancy over the full leg space, including collisions. Blocked use: ['Assume perverse exactness of arbitrary Hecke functors on arbitrary complexes.', 'Apply the moving-leg truncation comparison using constancy only away from collision diagonals.']
- **limit-betti-realization-and-universal-kernels** (formal_scope): Betti realization verifies comparisons for already defined geometric adic objects. The infinite-rank universal monodromic kernels are used on the Betti side and do not supply an adic realization of an arbitrary Betti object. Blocked use: ['Specialize an arbitrary infinite-rank Betti kernel as though it were a geometric adic locally bounded constructible complex.']
- **limit-radial-exchange-entire-family** (hypothesis): The radial reduction requires a torus torsor extending across the entire analytic family, followed by a proper map, with product-constructible inputs on the specified local charts. Blocked use: ['Apply radial exchange to a torsor defined only over the punctured disc.', 'Use the comparison for arbitrary sheaves that are not monodromic in the torus direction.']
- **limit-enhancement-pushforward-nonvanishing** (hypothesis): Forgetting an enhancement by compact-support direct image can annihilate a nonzero complex. The descent proof derives unipotent enhancement monodromy from boundary unipotence and the central trace identity before proving nonvanishing. Blocked use: ['Push an arbitrary nonzero enhanced-flag complex to ordinary flags and assert that it remains nonzero.', 'Replace individual-factor central monodromy compatibility by compatibility only with total monodromy.']
- **limit-nodal-type-and-branch-convention** (hypothesis): The nodal identification uses the prescribed alcove stabilizer type, opposite branch Borels, and the simultaneous-frame torus convention. Its quotient presentation does not discard the gluing identifications. Blocked use: ['Apply the enhanced-flag quotient identification to an arbitrary nodal stabilizer type.', 'Replace the quotient stack by a coarse moduli space or omit the simultaneous torus quotient.']
- **limit-finite-executable-construction-absent** (nonconstructive): The construction uses a categorical inverse image and compatible towers of finite torsors. A finite executable representation of the resulting eigensheaf, a terminating construction procedure, and computational complexity guarantees are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Advertise the existence proof as an implemented eigensheaf generator.', 'Treat finite checks of auxiliary linear algebra as certification of the global existence and coherence theorem.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct nonzero locally constructible perverse Hecke eigensheaves with Borel level at one marked point on a smooth projective curve of genus at least two over an algebraic closure of a finite field. The parameter is a Zariski-dense geometric <i>ℓ</i>-adic local system with tame regular-unipotent monodromy. The group is simple and simply connected and satisfies four explicit Lie-theoretic characteristic hypotheses. The eigensheaves have parabolic nilpotent singular support, and their eigenisomorphisms are compatible with tensor products, permutations, and fusion.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A Hecke eigensheaf realizes a local system on a curve through the geometry of modifications of bundles. For a punctured curve, the local monodromy of the parameter must also be reflected in the level structure at the puncture. We study Borel level and tame regular-unipotent monodromy. The required automorphic object is a locally constructible perverse sheaf, and its eigenisomorphisms must hold over the entire space of Hecke legs, including their collision diagonals.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The setting and the main theorem

Let $k=\overline{\mathbb F}_q$ have characteristic $p$, and let $E=\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell$, where $\ell\ne p$. Let $X_0/\mathbb F_q$ be a smooth projective geometrically connected curve of genus $g\ge2$, with a rational point $x_0$. Write $X=X_0\times_{\mathbb F_q}k$, $x=(x_0)_k$, and $U=X\setminus\{x\}$. Let $G_0/\mathbb F_q$ be split, simple, and simply connected of positive rank, and fix a Borel subgroup $B_0\subset G_0$. Put $G=(G_0)_k$, $B=(B_0)_k$, and $\mathfrak g=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(G)$. The dual group $\widehat G/E$ is adjoint.

We make the following assumptions on the characteristic. In the statements below, $M$ ranges over Levi subgroups of $G$, $\mathfrak m=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(M)$, $T_M\subset M$ is a maximal torus, and $W_M=N_M(T_M)/T_M$.

1.  <span id="hyp:form" label="hyp:form"></span> There is a nondegenerate $G$-invariant symmetric form $\kappa$ on $\mathfrak g$, and its restriction to the Lie-algebra center $\mathfrak z(\mathfrak m)$ is nondegenerate for every $M$.

2.  <span id="hyp:chevalley" label="hyp:chevalley"></span> Restriction induces an isomorphism $$k[\mathfrak m]^M\xrightarrow{\sim}k[\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(T_M)]^{W_M}
     \qquad\text{for every }M.$$

3.  <span id="hyp:centralizer" label="hyp:centralizer"></span> The scheme-theoretic centralizer in $G$ of every semisimple element of $\mathfrak g$ is a Levi subgroup.

4.  <span id="hyp:nilpotent" label="hyp:nilpotent"></span> For every field extension $k'/k$, each nilpotent element of $\mathfrak g\otimes_k k'$ belongs to $\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(R_u(P))$ for some parabolic subgroup $P\subset G_{k'}$ defined over $k'$.

Here semisimple means geometrically conjugate into a toral Lie algebra, and nilpotent means that the geometric adjoint-orbit closure contains zero. We impose no additional condition that $p$ be prime to the order of a Weyl group.

<a id="eq:parameter"></a>
<a id="eq:regular-monodromy"></a>
A parameter $\sigma$ is a continuous homomorphism $$\label{eq:parameter}
 \rho:\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(U,\bar u)\longrightarrow\widehat G(L),$$ up to $\widehat G(E)$-conjugacy, where $L/\mathbb Q_\ell$ is finite inside $E$. We require its image to be Zariski dense. It defines a tensor local system $V\mapsto V_\sigma$ for $V\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}_E(\widehat G)$. We further require that $\rho$ kill wild inertia at $x$ and that its tame inertia action factor through $\mathbb Z_\ell(1)$. After choosing a compatible system of $\ell$-power roots of unity, let $t_\ell:I_x\to\mathbb Z_\ell$ be the resulting quotient map. The regular-unipotent condition is $$\label{eq:regular-monodromy}
 \rho(\gamma)=\exp\bigl(t_\ell(\gamma)N\bigr),\qquad \gamma\in I_x,
 \qquad \dim Z_{\widehat G}(N)=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(\widehat G),$$ for a nilpotent $N\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(\widehat G)(L)$ after a finite extension of $L$ and a conjugation. The exponential is the finite nilpotent exponential. Changing the chosen generator rescales $N$ by a unit, so the condition does not add a generator or a logarithm to the automorphic data. No Frobenius descent is assumed for $\sigma$.

Let $$\mathcal A=\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_{G,B,x}(X)$$ be the stack of $G$-bundles $P$ on $X$ with a $B$-reduction $\beta$ at $x$. A geometric $E$-adic sheaf $M$ on $\mathcal A$ is *locally constructible perverse* if, for every smooth map $f:S\to\mathcal A$ from a smooth finite-type $k$-scheme of constant relative dimension $d$, the complex $f^*M[d]$ is bounded constructible and perverse. This condition is local on the bundle stack; it requires no bound on the Harder–Narasimhan strata supporting $M$.

Using $\kappa$, a cotangent vector to $\mathcal A$ is represented by $$(P,\beta,\phi),\qquad
 \phi\in H^0\bigl(X,\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}(P)\otimes\omega_X(x)\bigr),\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_x(\phi)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(R_u(B_\beta)).$$ The *parabolic global nilpotent cone* $\Lambda_{\mathrm{par}}$ consists of these triples for which $\phi$ is generically nilpotent. The singular-support condition below is interpreted by pulling this cone back to the cotangent bundle of each smooth scheme chart.

For a finite set $I$ and $V_i\in\mathop{\mathrm{Rep}}_E(\widehat G)$, let $\mathsf H_{I,(V_i)}$ denote the Hecke functor away from $x$, with target sheaves on $\mathcal A\times U^I$. We use the relative Satake normalization: on the open Schubert cell of dimension $d_\lambda$ the simple kernel is $E[d_\lambda](d_\lambda/2)$, with no additional moving-leg shift. These dimensions are even because $G$ is simply connected. In particular, the tensor-unit functor sends $M$ to $M\boxtimes E_{U^I}$. Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">2</a> fixes the corresponding tensor and fusion conventions.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Assume <a href="#hyp:form" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:form">[hyp:form]</a>–<a href="#hyp:nilpotent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hyp:nilpotent">[hyp:nilpotent]</a>. For every Zariski-dense parameter <a href="#eq:parameter" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameter">[eq:parameter]</a> satisfying <a href="#eq:regular-monodromy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:regular-monodromy">[eq:regular-monodromy]</a>, there is a nonzero locally constructible perverse geometric $E$-adic sheaf $M$ on $\mathcal A$ such that $$\relax(M)\subset\Lambda_{\mathrm{par}}.$$ For every finite set $I$ and every family $(V_i)_{i\in I}$, it carries isomorphisms <a id="eq:main-eigen"></a>$$\label{eq:main-eigen}
 \mathsf H_{I,(V_i)}(M)
 \simeq M\boxtimes\Bigl(\mathop{\boxtimes}_{i\in I}(V_i)_\sigma\Bigr)
 \quad\text{on }\mathcal A\times U^I,$$ natural in the representations and coherently compatible with the tensor unit, convolution, permutations of legs, and fusion along every collision diagonal.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Historical context

The eigensheaf formulation of geometric Langlands grew from Drinfeld’s rank-two construction and Laumon’s geometric formulation and proposed construction for general linear groups (Drinfeld 1983; Laumon 1987) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Drinfeld](#ref-Drinfeld), [Laumon](#ref-Laumon)]. Frenkel, Gaitsgory, and Vilonen reduced the unramified $\mathrm{GL}_n$ existence problem to a vanishing theorem (Frenkel, Gaitsgory, and Vilonen 2002) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[FGV](#ref-FGV)]; Gaitsgory proved that theorem (Gaitsgory 2004) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[GaitsgoryVanishing](#ref-GaitsgoryVanishing)]. For general reductive groups in characteristic zero, Beilinson and Drinfeld constructed eigensheaves from opers by quantizing the Hitchin system (Beilinson and Drinfeld, n.d., sec. 0.2). The tensor structure and fusion of geometric Hecke operators are furnished by geometric Satake (Mirković and Vilonen 2007, secs. 4–5,14) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[MV](#ref-MV)].

Ramified eigensheaves have also been studied through automorphic data with prescribed level and character. Yun’s rigidity framework constructs eigenvalues from suitable rigid automorphic data (Yun 2014, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[YunRigidity](#ref-YunRigidity)]; the present problem starts instead with a prescribed Zariski-dense local system. In the characteristic-zero de Rham setting, Færgeman constructs coherent eigensheaves for irreducible regular-singular parameters using the factorizable spectral input specified in his paper (Færgeman 2024, Theorem 1.4.1.1,§1.3.7) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Faergeman](#ref-Faergeman)]. In the version cited here, regular holonomicity and generic perversity in the ramified setting are left as expectations (Færgeman 2024, Remark 1.4.1.2) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Faergeman](#ref-Faergeman)]. Our theorem concerns geometric adic coefficients in positive characteristic, Borel level, and local constructibility together with perversity.

The geometry of the global nilpotent cone was developed by Laumon, Faltings, and Ginzburg (Laumon 1988; Faltings 1993; Ginzburg 2001) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[LaumonNilpotent](#ref-LaumonNilpotent), [Faltings](#ref-Faltings), [GinzburgNilpotent](#ref-GinzburgNilpotent)]. Beilinson established the singular-support theory for constructible etale sheaves, and Barrett extended it to the adic coefficient categories used here (Beilinson 2016; Barrett 2024) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Beilinson](#ref-Beilinson), [Barrett](#ref-Barrett)]. Arinkin, Gaitsgory, Kazhdan, Raskin, Rozenblyum, and Varshavsky developed the restricted spectral stack and its action on sheaves with nilpotent singular support (Arinkin et al. 2020) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. Their Hecke detection argument uses a central cocharacter of the Levi centralizing a semisimple Higgs value and an isolated cotangent intersection (Arinkin et al. 2020, secs. 20.5,20.7–20.8, Appendix H) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[AGKRRV](#ref-AGKRRV)]. The cotangent calculations below adapt these ideas to the level structures and the characteristic hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

Gaitsgory and Raskin establish the unramified restricted equivalence in characteristic zero and develop geometric specialization and its compatibility with the Hecke action (Gaitsgory and Raskin 2025, Main Theorem 1.3.9,§4) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR)]. We use their characteristic-zero equivalence to produce a compact eigencomplex, and prove the required level-dependent specialization and nonvanishing assertions here. The twisted nodal geometry belongs to the automorphic gluing framework of Nadler and Yun (Nadler and Yun 2021, secs. 2–3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[NYAutomorphicGluing](#ref-NYAutomorphicGluing)]. Their gluing theorem is a categorical construction; the nonvanishing of the particular specialized eigencomplex used below is a separate argument.

In Betti sheaf theory, Nadler and Yun construct a spectral action with level structure and prove local constancy of the moving Hecke operators on the nilpotent category (Nadler and Yun 2019, Theorems 6.2.2,6.3.7) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[NY](#ref-NY)]. At the marked point we use Gaitsgory’s nearby-cycle construction of central sheaves (Gaitsgory 2001) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[GaitsgoryCentral](#ref-GaitsgoryCentral)], the local spectral geometry of Arkhipov and Bezrukavnikov (Arkhipov and Bezrukavnikov 2009) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[AB](#ref-AB)], and its universal monodromic form due to Dhillon and Taylor (Dhillon and Taylor 2025) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[DT](#ref-DT)]. The latter allows arbitrary enhancement monodromy before the central trace calculation forces it to be unipotent.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The two specializations and the proof strategy

The proof first constructs an eigensheaf with Borel level in characteristic zero, and then specializes it to the curve of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. The characteristic-zero construction itself uses a different specialization: a degeneration to a separating node creates the level structure.

Start with a pointed complex curve and a dense parameter with unipotent boundary monodromy on its punctured complement. Join two copies of the curve at their marked points and smooth the resulting node. On the second punctured component, choose a parameter with inverse boundary monodromy. The compatible finite quotients of the two parameters glue on twisted nodal models and lift to the smooth generic curve. Their inverse limit is an unramified dense parameter there, to which we apply the characteristic-zero unramified correspondence. Taking the compact spectral skyscraper at this parameter gives a nonzero eigencomplex that is locally constructible.

The special fiber introduces enhanced flags. Put $T=B/R_u(B)$ and let $\mathcal A^+$ be the stack of bundles with an $R_u(B)$-reduction at the marked point. The map $q:\mathcal A^+\to\mathcal A$ is a $T$-torsor. For a suitable prescribed stabilizer type at the twisted node, the bundle stack is $$(\mathcal A_1^+\times\mathcal A_2^+)/T.$$ Nearby cycles, pullback to the product, and restriction to one enhanced factor give a nonzero eigencomplex on $\mathcal A_1^+$. Taking a nonzero perverse cohomology, applying $q_!$, and taking perverse cohomology once more produce the ordinary-flag perverse eigensheaf. The arguments below establish nonvanishing and preserve the eigenstructure at each of these steps.

To return to positive characteristic, lift the original pointed curve and the compatible finite quotients of its parameter to mixed characteristic. Apply the preceding construction on the geometric generic fiber. Its geometric nearby cycles give the sheaf on $\mathcal A$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. The two specializations are shown in Figure <a href="#fig:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:construction">1</a>.

<figure id="fig:construction">

<figcaption>The first specialization creates the level structure; the second changes the characteristic. The arrow from enhanced to ordinary flags takes perverse cohomology both before and after $q_{!}$. Each specialization also requires a separate nonvanishing argument. The arrows record operations on sheaves, not morphisms between the displayed moduli stacks.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-construction"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:construction`)

\begin{tikzpicture}[
 box/.style={draw,rounded corners=2pt,align=center,text width=3.5cm,
             minimum height=1.2cm,inner sep=5pt,font=\small},
 arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=2mm]},thick},
 lab/.style={font=\footnotesize,align=center}]
 \node[box] (unram) {Unramified eigencomplex\\on a smooth curve};
 \node[box,right=1.1cm of unram] (enh) {Eigencomplex\\on $\cA_1^+$};
 \node[box,right=1.1cm of enh] (ord) {Perverse eigensheaf\\on $\cA_1$};
 \draw[arr] (unram) -- node[lab,above=8mm]{nodal nearby cycles\\and restriction} (enh);

  2. Horizontal right arrow pointing to the second box, labeled above with "nodal nearby cycles and restriction"
  3. "Eigencomplex on $\mathcal{A}_1^+$"
  4. Horizontal right arrow pointing to the third box, labeled above with "perverse cohomology and $q_!$"
  5. "Perverse eigensheaf on $\mathcal{A}_1$"
- Vertical branch:
  1. Downward arrow from "Perverse eigensheaf on $\mathcal{A}_1$" to a fourth box below, labeled on the right with "mixed-characteristic nearby cycles"

The first difficulty is nonvanishing. Nearby cycles on a nonproper bundle stack can annihilate a nonzero object; at the node they must also be nonzero on the prescribed stabilizer type. Uniformization places a nonzero generic stalk in a bounded Hecke space proper over a reference bundle of that type. Its extension shows that the support approaches the required special fiber.
